Why United Airlines Accepts A 222-Seat Jet Over A Standard 257-Seat One On Its CES Tokyo Route

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

In January 2027, United Airlines will begin new nonstop flights between Tokyo Narita International Airport (NRT) and Las Vegas Harry Reid International Airport (LAS). This is an unusual launch on the surface, since LAS is not a major hub for the airline, but it is a limited service scheduled around the annual Consumer Electronics Show (CES), held from January 6 to January 9. The carrier will run three NRT-LAS flights on January 3, 4, and 5, and three LAS-NRT nonstop flights on January 9, 10, and 11.

The three NRT-LAS flights before the 2027 CES will use United's Boeing 777-200ER, featuring 276 seats and 50 Polaris seats. The three flights to Tokyo, however, will use the Boeing 787-9 rather than the 777. More interestingly, the airline will use its new Elevated 787-9 with its latest interiors and a premium-heavy configuration, featuring 64 Polaris suites and just 222 seats in total. It's unusual to use two different aircraft on the same route in opposite directions, especially when one has 54 more seats than the other.

While LAS is a large spoke for United, it's not a particularly high-priority market, and the CES services exist to better serve existing Star Alliance customers who need to travel from Asia to CES nonstop, helping retain customer loyalty. In addition, these services also help expand United's and All Nippon Airways' (the two operate a metal-neutral transpacific joint venture) potential customer base, as some customers may have booked away from the two in the past for CES due to the lack of a nonstop.

These routes work because of the sudden surge in demand to Las Vegas during this period, but they won't operate for a long period of time, which means United needs to find extra aircraft capable of operating the route. The 777 is coming from San Francisco, with United scheduling a second daily flight from San Francisco to NRT under the flight number UA 936 on January 2, 3, and 4. In NRT, the 777 turns around for UA 937 to LAS; after arrival, it operates a service to San Francisco again.

The return leg with the 787-9 works slightly differently. United has not scheduled a domestic leg to LAS with the Elevated 787-9, which means that the aircraft will need to be ferried. It then operates UA 7 to NRT, turns around, then operates UA 6 to IAH. Crucially, UA 7 is ordinarily used for the airline's outbound service from IAH to NRT, but this flight simply won't operate on the three days when the LAS-NRT flights are running. In contrast, United is adding a second 777 flight from SFO to NRT to position the aircraft for the NRT-LAS flight.

With these services, United is essentially using spare aircraft capacity, with the 777-200ER fleet having enough slack during those three days to operate a SFO-NRT-LAS-SFO rotation. Meanwhile, the airline felt that it was lucrative to cancel the outbound IAH-NRT leg for three days and use the Elevated 787-9 from LAS to NRT. Importantly, these decisions are being made based on what aircraft is available, not necessarily because the carrier needs to fly the 777-200ER or Elevated 787-9 on this service.

Every business decision comes with an opportunity cost. In the case of the LAS-NRT flight, United chose to cancel the IAH-NRT flight for three days and use that aircraft to operate the LAS-NRT flight, a decision weighed against pulling an aircraft from elsewhere. This doesn't necessarily mean that the IAH-NRT flight is particularly weak, but the math favored this solution above all others. In addition, the Elevated 787-9 could also be particularly desirable for this route.

The Elevated Interior and 222-seat layout debuted only in 2026, while January 2027 will be the first time United operates CES services. Primarily, these flights are about increasing customer loyalty and expanding the customer base, so United is putting its latest and greatest aircraft on this route. The Elevated 787-9 not only has the airline's newest seats, but it also has an extremely premium-heavy layout, and exposing these customers to the best United can offer further supports its mission.

Decisions like these involve multiple factors, including the IAH-NRT flight's performance on the selected days, the value of flying the Elevated 787-9 on LAS-NRT, the opportunity cost of pulling an aircraft from another route, and availability. It's also worth noting that mid-January generally has low travel demand in the US, which may explain why United could source a spare 777 and why United may have been inclined to cancel the IAH-NRT flight for three days. Of course, the airline has not confirmed these reasons.

Delta One and United Polaris are leading business class products, but which one is the better choice? Is network coverage better than luxury?

The Elevated 787-9 has significantly fewer seats than United's standard 787-9, but it has substantially more premium capacity. For the customer base booking flights for CES, the added Polaris and Premium Plus capacity is exactly what is desired, which means that yields will likely remain high while most of the seats will still be filled. As such, the carrier should earn a substantial amount of revenue from operating this route with the Elevated 787-9, even if economy capacity is low.

Although the Elevated 787-9 has significantly more premium seats than the Boeing 777-200ER, the 777 is still a very premium-heavy aircraft. With 50 Polaris seats and 24 Premium Plus seats, the 777-200ER has more premium capacity than the standard 787-9, and it is one of the most premium-heavy aircraft in United's fleet. The use of the 777-200ER demonstrates that the airline clearly saw strong premium demand, even if the return flight uses an even more premium-heavy aircraft.

Offering the Elevated 787-9 on this route may have been United's priority, but the aircraft may not have been available to pull from another service in both directions. This could be why the airline instead pulled a 777-200ER for the flights to LAS, but then took an Elevated 787-9 from the Houston route on days when demand may have been weaker. Because of the limited nature of this route, opportunity cost plays a much bigger factor.

United currently has 51 internationally configured 777-200ERs, while only 16 of its 64 787-9s are in the 222-seat layout with the Elevated Interior. There is a significant mismatch in capacity between the two segments, while premium capacity is actually much higher on the 787. United installs 50 Polaris seats, 24 Premium Plus seats, 46 Economy Plus seats, and 156 standard economy seats on the 777-200ER. Meanwhile, the Elevated 787-9 is equipped with 64 Polaris Suites, 35 Premium Plus seats, 39 Economy Plus seats, and just 84 economy seats.

Inside, the Elevated 787s feature the next generation of Polaris business class seats. Based on the Elevate Ascent, these seats are configured in a reverse-herringbone layout while featuring sliding doors. The screens are larger than the prior generation of Polaris seats, and the airline has configured half of the center seats in a herringbone pattern, creating honeymoon suites that can also partially convert into a double bed. Furthermore, these planes feature front-row 'Polaris Studio' suites.

United has also invested in the soft product of its cabins, including introducing new service features and amenities for the Polaris cabins. Meanwhile, the rest of the aircraft has been thoroughly updated compared to the airline's prior-generation widebody interiors. Premium Plus now offers headrest dividers for increased privacy, larger screens, new charging options, a water bottle holder, and a built-in reading light. Meanwhile, the economy seats come with larger screens and Bluetooth connectivity.

United's new premium-heavy layout on its Boeing 787-9 fleet is designed to compete with the world's best airlines.

American Airlines has long operated January-only transpacific services from Las Vegas for CES. In 2027, the airline will operate nonstop flights from LAS to NRT and Seoul Incheon International Airport (ICN), using a 273-seat 777-200ER on both flights in both directions. As with United's CES flights, American's service will operate only for a limited time, although it is significantly larger than United in this market.

While American is the more established player on CES routes from Asia, Delta Air Lines is growing quickly. In 2027, the carrier will run nonstop flights between LAS and ICN, Hong Kong International Airport (HKG), Shanghai Pudong International Airport (PVG), and Taipei Taoyuan International Airport (TPE). In addition, Delta is running nonstop flights to London Heathrow Airport (LHR), competing against American Airlines on this route, while also operating flights to Paris Charles de Gaulle Airport (CDG) and Amsterdam Schiphol Airport (AMS).

These services are all limited, although United is undoubtedly the small newcomer to this market. Overall, this is a small but lucrative market that US airlines are increasingly interested in, but only for a very short period.

4 Upcoming Fantasy RPGs Better Than Waiting for a New Dragon Age

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

The fire’s burning low. The road ahead looks grim. The companion who has spent half your journey needling you clearly has something on their mind, and you’re one or two correct dialogue choices away from getting them to spill. You pick a reply. You feel it land. Then somebody makes a joke about cheese, and the tension breaks.

This is one of the many things the Dragon Age franchise does well. In this series, from Origins to Veilguard, you gather a party of real-feeling companions and grapple with party management. These are also tactical games — games where you need to pause your combat encounters, point at the biggest threat, and hope against hope that your mage remembers she’s standing in a pool of fire. They’re political games, full of nobles and templars quibbling about the fates of their respective territories and teams. And they’re games where consequences stick. A choice you made in a prologue will come back to bite you hours (or entire games) later, usually while you’re just minding your own business collecting elfroot for a salve.

It may be unlikely that we’ll see a new Dragon Age title anytime soon. (Earlier this month, former Dragon Age writer David Gaider said that “the golden days are over.”) Yet there are still campfires worth stopping at. If you miss that feeling of saving Thedas by day and hanging out with your party of oddball companions by night, check out these four upcoming RPGs. Consider this your recruitment screen.

You can never keep every companion happy in Dragon Age. Solasta 2 eases you into this never-ending quest for balance by starting you off with your family. You lead the Colwall siblings, orphans from a household tied to a religious order sworn against an evil called Shadwyn. Soon, the siblings travel to a new continent, where the opening city, Caer Mar, comes with rebels, factions, and plenty of trouble.

If you liked pausing Dragon Age to plan every fight, this turn-based tactical RPG (which has been in Steam Early Access since March 2026) offers plenty of occasions to plan. Positioning, line of sight, and high ground decide most battles, and D&D fans will appreciate the chance to use spells like Magic Missile and Shatter to deal damage. Between fights, as you trek your way across Neokos, your role-playing choices will influence your standing with the world’s factions — which, in turn, changes how the world responds to you. Cross the wrong faction, and the merchants may stop selling you the good stuff.

Solasta 2’s Early Access only covers part of Act 1 and comes with a low level cap — you can only get to level 4 at this time. Developer Tactical Adventures says it intends to stay in Early Access for about a year, and 1.0 will raise the cap to 10 and finish the main questline. While you wait, you can play the first game, Solasta: Crown of the Magister. It’s a crunchier D&D experience, so brush up on your spell strategy before you boot it up.

What if the Dragon Age: Origins prologue cast you as a vagrant with a bad attitude and no plan? In Outward 2, you play an ordinary person in Aurai, a dangerous world where most people never leave their hometown walls. You’ll choose one of three starting scenarios — a miner in the northern mountains of Tramontane, a homeowner in the underground city of Haboob, or a vagrant in Simeon’s Bastion. Then, you’ll get a background and personality traits like Hoarder, Gourmet, or Lone Wolf that change your dialogue, starting gear, and aptitudes.

Surviving Aurai will take more than a sword. Hunger, thirst, exhaustion, and weather threaten you as much as the world’s creatures do…and you don’t get mounts or fast travel to give you a quick way out of a tight bind. This game is more committed to realism (read: suffering) than your usual RPG. Armor weight will change how fast you move. If you want to stay mobile during a fight, you’ll need to drop your backpack. You can wield a different weapon in each hand, like a sword and shield, dual axes, or a mace and pistol, and trainers teach skills for a fee. But you can’t just be focused on survival: Your choices will pull you toward a faction that will shape your story. Will you join a budding mercenary company, a society of historians, or a humanitarian mission? What’ll happen after you do?

Outward 2 will support online and split-screen co-op, which means your companions can also be your IRL friends. While you wait for this title, play the first Outward if you like your fantasy with a side of hypothermia. Regardless, bring a friend. And a warm cloak, because the seasons in Aurai show no mercy.

Release date: Feb. 23, 2027 (Feb. 18 with the Premium Edition)

Where to play: PS5, Windows PC, Xbox Series X

This game’s not out yet, but Albion already has opinions about you. Here, you play as the hero of Briar Hill, “an ordinary person made extraordinary by fate.” From there, it’s up to you to decide what kind of hero you’ll be. Your built-in rival is Isabel, played by Hayley Atwell, a driven, powerful hero on a quest to right a tragic injustice. She opposes you and Humphry, her former guardian. With interpersonal conflict this charged, who needs a world to save?

Dragon Age taught us that the world should remember what you did, and Albion takes the lesson seriously. Your reputation affects how the world’s 1,000+ handcrafted NPCs treat you. That reputation can reshape the world around you and grow your power. Beyond reputation, you can romance villagers, have children with them, and start your own cozy life while chaos reigns above you.

Dragon Age blends dark subject matter with humor very well. Fable seems ready to carry on that particular mission. One of its spells, “Befowl,” turns enemies into chickens (and undead enemies into undead chickens, naturally). Isabel has an injustice to right and you’re standing in her way, so keep that chicken spell handy.

A weekly cheat sheet to what you’ll eventually love to play. No buying, no commitment — just saving for later.

If you ever wished you could replay your first Dragon Age campaign and fix just one decision, Clockwork Revolution might be your invitation to do just that. Dragon Age gave us choices that follow you from game to game. InXile’s first-person RPG explicitly lets you rewrite your own decisions to answer the question: Would anything change if you could reverse something from your past?

You play Morgan Vanette, a customizable member of the Rotten Row Hooligans gang in Avalon, a steampunk city whose factions run from the slums to the golden mansions. The villain, Lady Ironwood, already rules Avalon, and she’s been using time travel to keep it that way. Vanette and a band of allies set out to turn back the clock and right her wrongs.

Sounds standard, right? The twist, here, is how you go about doing that. You’ll use a device called the Chronometer to change key moments in the past. This sounds excellent in terms of instant gratification, but the effects of those changes threaten to ripple outward. The people closest to you feel it first, and the crew you return to after time shenanigans may differ from the one you left. Your allies, Ulysses, Nazim, Erasmus, Hazel, and Anne, have lives of their own, with stories that don’t revolve around you. If you enjoy political chess, Lady Ironwood gives you a rival to follow. A flying automaton named Prentice will guide you through the branching realities. You’ll build a character with attributes, skills, and gear, hack hostile automatons, and use an ability called Displace to toss an explosive barrel at enemies. Whatever you do, just change the past carefully, because your crew will remember.

A distant exoplanet mysteriously vanished from sight. Astronomers just discovered why

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Join The Club for quick access. Enter your email below and we'll send confirmation, and sign you up to our newsletter.

Keep exploring and earning more as a member.

Sign up to our monthly entertainment newsletter to keep up with all our coverage of the latest sci-fi and space movies, tv shows, games and books.

Discover this week's must-see night sky events, moon phases, and stunning astrophotos. Sign up for our skywatching newsletter and explore the universe with us!

Space.com's Sci-Fi Reader's Club. Read a sci-fi short story every month and join a virtual community of fellow science fiction fans!

Get full access to premium articles, exclusive features and a growing list of member rewards.

"We think of the universe as resembling clockwork, where everything is regular, but that's not true."

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

Astronomers have solved the mystery of an extrasolar planet, or exoplanet, that "disappeared" after seven years of observations. New research suggests that the planet hasn't vanished at all, but has been dragged off its orbit by an unseen planet.

The exoplanet, designated TOI-2535 b, is located around 1,000 light-years away. TOI-2535 b was routinely observed with NASA's Transiting Exoplanet Survey Satellite (TESS) space telescope for seven years between April 2019 and March 2026 as it completed its 8.25-Earth day orbit of its parent star. That's 10 times quicker than the orbit of Mercury around the sun and means this blisteringly hot planet is classed as a "hot Jupiter."

However, something strange happened started to happen as astronomers observed the distant world. The orbits of the planet weren't regular like those of other planets, with TOI-2535 b often arriving six hours early or six hours later than when astronomers expected. Then, in March this year, TESS was no longer spotting the characteristic drops in starlight that TOI-2535 b caused as it crossed, or "transited," the face of its parent star. It seemed to have vanished.

This team used TESS data to determine that TOI-2535 b is being pulled on by an unseen neighbor and is, in turn, pulling on that planet as well. This is causing the orbits of both worlds in the TOI-2535 system to "wobble." TOI-2535 b disappeared when that wobble tilted it out of alignment with its star.

"We think of the universe as resembling clockwork, where everything is regular, but that's not true. If there are multiple planets in a system, they can affect each other's orbits. We know of a few systems that are like this," team member Samuel Yee of the University of California, Los Angeles, said in a statement. "This is the most extreme example we've seen because the two planets are interacting so strongly. The planet's orbit is tilting so quickly that its transit disappeared while we were watching."

TOI-2535 b isn't expected to transit its parent star for at least 30 years, but the hidden neighbor tugging on it may cross the face of the star sooner than that.

In the meantime, scientists will attempt to decode what this extreme wobbling exoplanet system says about how it formed.

There is currently a lot of speculation about how hot Jupiter planets like TOI-2535 b form. That is because current routes of planet formation suggest planets like this would need to form around a great deal of ice. Obviously, ice isn't likely to be abundant as close to a star as TOI-2535 b is currently.

This means that hot Jupiters likely form far from their stars and then migrate inwards. There are two ways that this could happen. The planet could be excited into an elliptical orbit by violent events like a near-collision with another planetary object, and then interactions with the star pull the planet inwards.

Alternatively, the hot Jupiter may be pulled inwards along with the other planets while sitting in a flattened cloud of gas and dust around the infant star called a protoplanetary disk.

If this second, less turbulent route occurs to bring hot Jupiters close to their stars, then the resultant system should be very flat. In other words, there shouldn't be extreme tilts between the orbits of the planets in that system.

However, the tilt caused to TOI-2535 b by its unseen companion indicates that flattened systems can be twisted by more tilted orbits later in their existence. This makes the slower migration possible despite the orbital tilt.

"Because TOI-2535 b has a close neighbor, it probably migrated by slowly spiraling in instead of getting flung in," Yee said. "We may be seeing a formation channel that doesn’t happen very often but is very interesting."

The team's research was published on Monday (Nov. 9) in the Astrophysical Journal Letters.

Robert Lea is a science journalist in the U.K. whose articles have been published in Physics World, New Scientist, Astronomy Magazine, All About Space, Newsweek and ZME Science. He also writes about science communication for Elsevier and the European Journal of Physics. Rob holds a bachelor of science degree in physics and astronomy from the U.K.’s Open University. Follow him on Twitter @sciencef1rst.

Shaw Contract Design Awards name top interiors of 2026

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

A Japanese office intended to evoke a tree canopy, and a revived US concert hall are among the interior design projects to win Best of Globe at this year’s Shaw Contract Design Awards.

The upgrade of an athletics clubhouse in the US, a legal workplace in the UK and a large three-story office in India also took the top prize in the annual award programme, now in its 21st year.

Held annually by flooring manufacturer Shaw Contract, the Shaw Contract Design Awards celebrate interior design projects that “spark creativity, connect communities and contribute to a better, more sustainable world”.

This year’s winning projects were selected from nearly 550 global submissions by an international jury of architects and designers, with interior design students also joining as part of Shaw’s commitment to supporting the next generation of design talent.

According to the jury, the 2026 winners were unified by “a clear design narrative, an understanding of local context, varied and inclusive user experiences, responsible material strategies and a meaningful relationship between design intent and completed space” – despite their varying scales and styles.

“Throughout the global judging session, the jury returned to a central question: whether each project’s stated intent was visible and understandable in the completed design,” explained Shaw Contract.

“The panel distinguished between attractive spaces and projects that used planning, materials, detail and experience to communicate a clear and consequential idea.”

The St Louis Symphony Orchestra Powell Hall in the US, overhauled by Christner Architects, was one of the winners.

According to Shaw Contract, the jury selected it for its sensitive balance of old and new elements, as well as the important social role it plays in the city, thanks to improved accessibility and audience facilities.

Also in the US is the winning Missouri Athletic Club project, completed by HOK. The scheme involved revamping an existing clubhouse to better suit the needs of its current members.

HOK’s design was commended for adding “a new layer to the club’s heritage rather than treating the past as a fixed visual theme”, according to the brand, introducing a contemporary layout and aesthetic.

The third winner was We Labo 25F, a small workspace in Japan that embodies the principles of biophilic design – the use of natural features such as plants to improve the wellbeing of building occupants.

Its architect, Okamura, was praised for incorporating greenery as more than just “a decorative layer” and creating an interior that mimics working beneath a tree canopy.

The office was also selected in recognition of its adaptable interiors and circular design features, including furniture made from tsunami debris.

We Labo 25F was one of three workplaces to win Best of Globe, alongside Diageo by M Moser Associates in India and DLA Piper by Sheppard Robson Architects in the UK.

Diageo spans 7,700 square metres and accommodates 300 employees across each of its three floors, accommodating its owner’s expansion from Bengaluru to Gurugram.

It was selected by the jury for its “richly layered and carefully crafted” interiors, which combine a wide range of workspaces with shared hospitality areas. M Moser Associates dedicated more than half of the building to community spaces.

Finally, DLA Piper by Sheppard Robson Architects is a large workplace for a legal company. It was designed to cater to different work styles.

According to Shaw Contract, it won the prize for providing a broad range of workspaces suited to different needs, as well as wellbeing, dining and meeting areas.

The jury particularly enjoyed how materials had been applied in a way that adds “warmth and richness into areas that might traditionally be treated as purely corporate”.

The Best of Globe winners are selected from the programme’s Best of Region winners. Winners receive global media exposure and a donation to a charity of their choice.

Shaw Contract’s ambition for the award programme is to encourage discourse within the international creative community.

To learn more about Shaw Contract and the awards programme, visit its website here.

MARA Sells 13,597 Bitcoin for $1.1B as Its AI Pivot Reshapes Treasury Strategy

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Strict editorial policy that focuses on accuracy, relevance, and impartiality

Morbi pretium leo et nisl aliquam mollis. Quisque arcu lorem, ultricies quis pellentesque nec, ullamcorper eu odio.

MARA has put a sizeable number on the changing economics of Bitcoin mining. In its third-quarter shareholder letter, the company said it sold 13,597 BTC after September 30 for approximately $1.1 billion, at an average selling price of about $81,039. It used $750 million of the proceeds to repay Bitcoin-collateralized loans, releasing 17,477 BTC that had been pledged as security.

TL;DR: MARA disclosed $1.1 billion of post-quarter Bitcoin sales and repayment of $750 million in BTC-backed borrowing. The miner is increasingly monetizing its coin reserves to support AI infrastructure and other capital priorities. This marks a meaningful departure from its earlier strategy of holding mined Bitcoin.

At the end of September, MARA reported 34,012 BTC in total holdings, including a sizeable portion lent out or pledged as collateral. The newly disclosed disposals followed quarter-end, so that September balance should not be presented as the company’s current holding without adjustment. MARA said the repayments reduced the proportion of Bitcoin lent or pledged to less than 5% of its remaining holdings, versus roughly 57% at the earlier reporting date.

Management describes the move as a way to gain financial flexibility while still using Bitcoin strategically. Borrowing against the coins had helped finance the proposed acquisition of the Long Ridge natural-gas power business. The subsequent sale allowed MARA to clear the borrowing before the transaction closed, instead of leaving its financing exposed to future collateral calls if Bitcoin weakened.

That is a trade-off, not a free improvement. Selling reserve assets reduces direct exposure to any subsequent Bitcoin recovery. Keeping more BTC would preserve that upside but could require accepting additional liquidity and borrowing risk. The appropriate decision depends on the company’s priorities and its ability to earn a return on the infrastructure being funded.

MARA’s third-quarter revenue fell about 35% from a year earlier to $164.2 million. Its mining operations still produce coins, but the company is now emphasizing power assets and potential data-center customers alongside hashrate. Long Ridge includes generating capacity and land that could support a data-center campus. Development plans are not the same as signed, revenue-producing leases.

The move also fits a wider sector pattern. NewsBTC recently reported how Riot sold more than 20,000 BTC while pursuing AI-related data-center agreements. The contrast with Strive’s rapid Bitcoin accumulation is useful: public companies can use the same underlying asset in radically different corporate strategies.

MARA also disclosed authorization for up to $500 million in share repurchases, while its reported quarterly profit included substantial accounting effects linked to digital assets. Those items should not be mistaken for recurring mining cash flow. Nor does an authorization guarantee that every dollar of the program will be spent.

The follow-up questions are whether Long Ridge closes, whether planned data-center leases are signed, how much Bitcoin remains unrestricted, and whether new AI revenue eventually justifies the capital committed. The BTC sale is confirmed. The return on the assets it may finance is still an execution story.

Sony Cancels Fairgames After Years of Work on PS5 Live Service Shooter

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

PlayStation has officially cancelled Fairgames, the PS5 and PC live service shooter it had in production at Haven Studio.

The news comes from Bloomberg journalist Jason Schreier, though it's been sourced from an internal email distributed to PlayStation employees, and a company spokesperson has commented on the story.

That spokesperson told the outlet that Haven Studio will remain open following the cancellation, and it won't suffer any job losses. The developer, originally headed by Jade Raymond of Assassin's Creed fame — who left about a year and a half ago — will now become a support team within PlayStation Studios.

In the email to PlayStation employees, it's said a recent test of Fairgames didn't deliver the results the company wanted.

It said: "While the test demonstrated strong operational excellence, live ops readiness and community engagement, player feedback and retention results were mixed and fell short of the goals we had set. Combined with challenging market conditions in the PVP shooter genre, we have made the difficult decision to cease development on the game."

Announced more than three years ago, Fairgames was one of the headline announcements from the infamous PlayStation Showcase that properly pushed Sony's live service efforts — it was also where Concord and Helldivers 2 debuted.

Since then, absolutely nothing official from the game has been made public except for studio departures.

The game has reportedly done poorly during pre-launch and private tests, which has now been confirmed in the email to employees this week. There were rumours Fairgames would be brought back with a new name, Break In, around the summer season of games conferences earlier this year, but it never materialised.

It's yet another failure from PlayStation's first-party efforts during the PS5 era, which has seen many cancellations to the likes of The Last of Us Online, Payback from Bungie, live service titles from Sony Bend and Bluepoint Games, and Twisted Metal. You might as well throw Concord in there too, because while it did actually release, it was taken offline permanently after just two weeks.

Let's hope the PS6 generation doesn't follow in these footsteps.

What is the 'Dead Internet Theory' and should humanity be worried?

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

When spending time online today, do you ever get the feeling that digital life has become eerily less human? Perhaps social media posts seem a bit off, or you get the sneaking suspicion the customer service representative you are chatting with is actually a bot. If this sounds familiar, you aren't alone: a paradigm called the "dead internet theory" has been gaining momentum in the last several years, positing that the internet is increasingly dominated by non-human activity, including bots and AI-generated content.

Since the theory first started making the rounds on internet forums in the 2010s, AI has only gotten more powerful. In September 2026, OpenAI reported that it had found a solution to a famously difficult mathematics puzzle, the Navier-Stokes problem. Meanwhile, a 2026 report from cybersecurity group Thales found that automated traffic continues to overtake human activity online, with bots accounting for 53 percent of all web traffic, up from 51 percent the year before. And bots are now talking to each other without humans in the loop at all: In March, Meta acquired Moltbook — a social network on which AI chatbots communicate with one another, often discussing humans. So as the internet landscape potentially gets less human every day, what exactly is dead internet theory, where does it come from, and should we be worried?

Dead Internet Theory — the idea that the majority of the internet is propelled by bots and other non-human agents – has been floating around for quite some time. It first emerged as a conspiracy theory on internet forums in the late 2010s, but gained a more concrete framework following a post in 2021 on online community Agora Road titled, "Dead Internet Theory: Most of the Internet Is Fake," which argued "the Internet feels empty and devoid of people." Followers of the theory argued that the internet "died" around 2016 when search engines and social media feeds started surfacing more AI-generated content.

The popularity of chatbots and AI-generated content has only further expanded since then. In 2024, only 33 percent of Americans said they had used chatbots like ChatGPT, Gemini or Copilot, a study from Pew Research found. By 2026, that number had increased to nearly half of Americans at 49 percent. That same report found that 25 percent of Americans use chatbots every day. In many realms of society we are now facing chatbot loops: in schools, students are writing essays using AI, which teachers then grade with AI. In the job search, employees use chatbots to spit out optimized resumes and cover letters which are then analyzed by AI systems. Increasingly, humans are cut out of the conversation entirely, our voices replaced with bots.

On a personal level, this chatbot loop can feel uncanny. Those script-like posts we see on LinkedIn or a soulless AI-generated poster at your local cafe can induce a feeling of existential dread, but the consequences go beyond personal discomfort. Bots can spread disinformation that causes real harm. AI chatbots are increasingly a source of election disinformation, and little is being done legislatively to regulate this new frontier, though government agencies have issued warnings on deepfakes and other AI-powered risks to free elections.

As AI use grows, so has the backlash: 40 percent of Americans say they think AI will have a negative impact on society in the next 20 years, according to a Pew Research study. AI detection is becoming increasingly popular, with company Pangram receiving $9 million in backing in July 2026 for its AI detection system. Following the passage of the EU AI Act, OpenAI has started watermarking AI-generated content. With measures like these, people opposed to AI will hopefully be able to more easily spot and avoid the artificial slop in favor of the human-centric connection they initially came to the internet to find.

Henry Rollins’ Latest Chapter? Punk Rock Archivist

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Inside a moderately-sized building in Nashville’s gentrifying-too-fast Wedgewood-Houston neighborhood, Henry Rollins confirms the rumblings that have been circulating in town: He is in fact a Music City resident. And he’s intent on giving back.

Over two days last weekend, Rollins unveiled “Cool Evidence: Captured Moments From the Last Century,” a curated exhibit featuring select items from his personal punk-rock history collection. Free to the public, the event at the venue Radar615 drew lines a few hundred people deep, each of them eager to lay eyes on relics from a niche but impactful musical past. There were gig posters for Minor Threat, rare photographs of Iggy Pop, movie posters that inspired the Misfits, given to him by Glenn Danzig, and the Häagen-Dazs shirt that Rollins himself wore at his last straight job slinging ice cream.

“I guard all of this very jealously,” Rollins tells Rolling Stone during a friends, family, and media preview the day before the public opening. He says he wanted “Cool Evidence” to feel like a 15-year-old boy’s bedroom wall meets the Smithsonian, and he hoped it would overwhelm guests, leaving them disoriented as they walked away.

Rollins moved to Nashville five years ago, after four decades in Los Angeles, and initially bought a building a few blocks from Radar615 to house his collection and serve as de facto Rollins HQ. But after trying to navigate bureaucratic red tape, and at his accountant’s urging, he sold it. That building has since been demolished, but Rollins’ exhibit teems with punk-rock energy.

The Black Flag singer and leader of Rollins Band developed an appreciation for collecting as a teen in Washington, D.C., with his friend Ian MacKaye, future singer for Minor Threat/Fugazi and co-proprietor of Dischord Records. “I’m not trying to mythologize myself, but we’re these two last good men standing, in that we don’t lie. We don’t rip you off,” Rollins, clad in a black shirt, black Dickies work pants, and white On Cloud sneakers, says. “We can finish a sentence because we never got high, and we’re not dead yet.” Editor’s picks The 250 Greatest Albums of the 21st Century So Far The 100 Best TV Episodes of All Time The 500 Greatest Albums of All Time 100 Best Movies of the 21st Century

While putting up flyers as a teen for MacKaye’s band Teen Idles, they witnessed someone tear one down. Rollins says he felt as if the vandal was “trying to erase our history,” so he started keeping three of everything: copied cassette tapes, Bad Brains flyers, set lists. “What, you’re not going to take that?” he asks, incredulously.

As his reputation as a historian grew, Rollins, who describes himself as a “stuff person,” became the archivist for others, including the Cramps, which led him and MacKaye to unearth the punk-rockabilly band’s 1977 shelved album, produced by Alex Chilton. “Cool Evidence” prominently features the Cramps, via a busted drumhead, a handwritten sign for the group’s show at the Marquee in London, and assorted posters.

All of the artifacts that adorn the walls are encased in plexi-archival framing, which, according to Rollins, will last 100 years. But the framing, done locally, wasn’t cheap: Rollins estimates the whole endeavor will cost him six figures. “[The framers] see me and they’re like, ‘Oh my God, turkey dinner,'” he jokes. “But it feels vulgar to talk about money.”

The exhibit includes distinct sections that pay tribute to Rollins’ own history with punk, namely Black Flag, which he joined in 1981. There’s a photo of the band when they were called Panic and featured Keith Morris on vocals, along with a few original Raymond Pettibon drawings. But it’s the Stooges section that’s the most fascinating, thanks to Rollins’ status as the preeminent collector of the band’s items. “There’s hardly anything you can get now because no Stooges fan gets anything. It’s from my cold dead hands,” he says. Related Content How Dropkick Murphys Became Punk’s Loudest Anti-Trump Voice Haywire Are Living Their Hardcore Dreams Punk Rock Is Heating Up Across the Caribbean X-Ray Spex, the Raincoats, the Slits Members Form Supergroup for London Punk Festival

In the exhibit, Polaroids and other items obtained from Michael Tipton — who recorded the Stooges’ 1976 live album Metallic K.O., the masters of which Rollins now owns and wants to re-release — are on display. There’s an extremely rare photo of Pop putting on makeup before a 1968 show in Ann Arbor, Michigan, the only time the Stooges singer did so. Out-of-focus images of the band from Tipton in the early Seventies are also on display. “Who gets backstage with the Stooges while Iggy’s getting his hair together with an Aquanet?” Rollins says.

Seeing the exhibit come to life, and as organized as it is, is exactly what Rollins dreamed up while running on a gym treadmill years ago. “I’ve been thinking of this for years, and now I get to see what my idea looks like in the physical world,” he says. “It’ll take me probably another three years to figure out if this will work or not. I think the ingredients are awesome.”

As for what’s next, Rollins says he’s done performing music. “I’m not going out to do the first album again. I’ll have a heart attack,” he says. “Four wheezing men onstage – it’s pathetic.” He has a book coming out next March, which will take up most of 2027, and he may start teaching a class at Vanderbilt University. Trending Stories Paul McCartney and Ringo Starr to Reunite at Las Vegas Show in 2027 Watch Ariana Grande, Ben Stiller, Robert De Niro Take Over ‘The Tonight Show’ Pink Becomes First Solo Female Pop Artist to Headline the Sphere Judge Sets Steven Tyler Sex Assault Trial Date

Most importantly, however, he’s optimistic about taking “Cool Evidence” on tour. Honoring the history of punk has become something of a calling for Rollins.

“Fuck it, this is what I like to do,” he says. “I’m 65. I’m in the fourth quarter. I want to be a museum guy, an archive guy, a ‘give you your stuff’ guy, and my two primary fields of study: Stooges and the Cramps. I like reinvention — I’ve always benefited when I’ve stopped doing one thing.”

The Hot 100 flows through social media, so Billboard is bringing YouTube data back into its charts

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Billboard and YouTube have kissed and made up. A disagreement between the two companies kept the latter out of the former’s chart calculus for about a year, but Billboard is adjusting its weights to give more recognition to ad-supported streams that occur on YouTube. As a result, YouTube data will be incorporated into the Billboard charts (again) in 2027 and beyond.

Lyor Cohen, YouTube’s Global Head of Music, announced the new policy in a blog post. “We are returning to the Billboard charts starting in 2027,” Cohen wrote. “Billboard’s new methodology better reflects how fans engage with music today across both subscription and ad-supported services.”

YouTube views first became part of Billboard‘s rankings in 2013, when the music magazine recognized the growing influence of digital streams across its charts. As soon as the single-based Hot 100 and album-based 200 began factoring in YouTube-based plays, the platform’s impact became abundantly clear. At the time, Baauer’s “Harlem Shake” was the soundtrack to an inescapable viral trend, and the EDM banger shot to the top of the Hot 200 as a result.

Daily industry updates, straight to your inbox.

Soon, the Hot 100 was filled with songs that were big on YouTube, and that led detractors to argue that Billboard was overemphasizing ad-supported streams. Record companies and fans could use repetitive videos to push their favorite songs to the top of the charts — and that’s exactly what they did.

In a 2017 adjustment to its chart policies, Billboard decided to weigh paid streams more heavily than ad-supported streams. As of 2025, it took 2.5 streams on an ad-supported service like YouTube to equal one stream on a subscription-based service like Spotify Premium.

Cohen protested that policy, and the squabbles eventually compelled YouTube to pull its data from the Billboard charts. “Every play should count equally,” Cohen wrote at the time.

Notably, Billboard‘s updated policy does not count all streams equally. Paid streams are still given more weight, though the gap has shrunk. Now, one paid stream will equal 1.5 ad-supported streams. That breakdown, according to Billboard, “better reflects the varied user activity occurring on these services.”

Billboard wants to do right by all its partners, but its charts couldn’t ignore YouTube forever. The platform’s impact on music consumption is undeniable, even if some of its streams only last for a few seconds. So when the Billboard charts come out on January 16, they will better reflect the pop culture landscape as it currently looks.

Fairgame$ canceled

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Sony Interactive Entertainment has canceled development subsidiary Haven Interactive Studios‘ May 2023-announced multiplayer shooter Fairgame$, according to a Bloomberg report.

A spokesperson for Sony Interactive Entertainment told Bloomberg there will be no job cuts as a result of the cancellation, and that Haven Interactive Studios will move to support other studios within the organization.

In an email to employees today, which was reviewed by Bloomberg, Sony Interactive Entertainment executives Sean Decker and John Doyle said a recent playtest with thousands of players—likely the “Project Espresso” playtest we reported on in September—did not deliver on the results the organizations expected.

“While the test demonstrated strong operational excellence, live ops readiness, and community engagement, player feedback and retention results were mixed and fell short of the goals we had set,” the email reads. “Combined with challenging market conditions in the [player-versus-player] shooter genre, we have made the difficult decision to cease development on the game.”

Fairgame$ was planned for release on PlayStation 5 and PC.

Remodeling Activity Holds Flat in Q2 Amid Housing Slowdown

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

The National Association of Home Builders (NAHB) reported that remodeling activity was flat in the second quarter amid a broader nationwide housing slowdown, in its latest State Projections of Remodeling (SPR).

The SPR showed a near-even split in the second quarter: Remodeling spending declined in 26 states and increased in 24 states, according to a release. The average growth rate across all states broke even at 0.0%.

“These flat results are consistent with NAHB’s forecast for remodeling spending in 2026,” NAHB Chief Economist Robert Dietz said. “Second-quarter spending growth was concentrated in smaller states. Among the 10 states with the largest market shares, only North Carolina and Illinois recorded growth.”

“Rising construction costs and weak consumer sentiment are weighing on projects and keeping spending flat, but the remodeling sector has solid long-term growth potential,” Elliott Pike—NAHB Remodelers chairman—stated. “An aging housing stock, the mortgage rate lock-in effect, home equity gains and more home owners choosing to age in place will support future growth.”

Remodeler confidence has also held steady. The release noted that NAHB’s Remodeling Market Index (RMI)—which measures confidence in the sector—has remained above 60 for the past year.

California led the nation in remodeling spending in the second quarter of 2026, accounting for about 8% of the market. The five largest state markets by share and dollar volume were:

The five states with the largest dollar increases in remodeling spending in the second quarter were:

As reported by the release, NAHB expects remodeling to grow in the years ahead as rising home equity helps more home owners fund projects, including upgrades that allow them to age in place. Remodeling also accounts for a growing share of residential construction, both in the number of firms and total construction spending. At the start of 2025, there were 128,000 remodeling firms, up from 69,000 in 2000.

Elon Musk Is Pulling the Plug on Cybertrucks to Focus on Something New

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Tesla plans to wind down Cybertruck production next year as Elon Musk shifts attention to its Cybercab. Here's what the change means.

Tesla Cybertrucks have dominated headlines even before they hit the road. But now it appears their time in the automotive world spotlight might be coming to an end. The company’s CEO Elon Musk says Tesla will wind down Cybertruck production at its Texas factory to make room for the Cybercab, the company’s self-driving taxi.

Learn why the company is changing direction and what the news means for the Cybertruck.

Austin American-Statesman/Hearst Newspapers/Getty Images

In a Nov. 6 post on X, Musk wrote, “Tesla will be winding down production of the Cybertruck at Gigafactory Texas around the middle of next year to make room for increased Cybercab production.”

Tesla began delivering Cybertrucks in late 2023, but sales haven’t lived up to expectations. According to Kelley Blue Book estimates published by Cox Automotive, U.S. sales fell by nearly half in 2025. The truck has also faced criticism for its price, design, and quality issues, as well as several safety recalls.

Current owners can continue driving their Cybertrucks, and existing warranty terms still apply. Cybertruck lead engineer Wes Morrill also addressed concerns about future repairs. “Tesla will maintain spare parts and service will continue support,” he wrote on X on November 6. He didn’t specify how long that support would last.

For anyone still waiting on a Cybertruck, the news is less encouraging. In a Nov. 9 post on X, Musk wrote, “We will try to make as many as possible before shutting down the line in about 8 months, but will have to return deposits for a lot of people.”

Tesla hasn’t said which customers will receive their trucks or how it plans to handle the remaining orders.

The Cybercab is Tesla’s two-seat electric car designed to drive itself. It doesn’t have a steering wheel or pedals because it won’t need a human driver. Tesla has already started offering Cybercab rides in parts of Austin. The company plans to expand the service, although the vehicles aren’t widely available yet.

Musk denies that Tesla is ending production because the Cybertruck isn’t profitable. He says the company can put its Texas factory to better use by making Cybercabs.

Oxford University Museum Returns 39 Sets of Ancestral Remains to Naga People of India

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

At a ceremony held on November 9, the Pitt Rivers Museum at Oxford University in the UK formally returned 39 sets of ancestral remains to the Naga people of India. The remains, primarily human skulls, were taken during the British colonial rule of India and Burma (now Myanmar) and have been in the museum’s collection for more than a century. A delegation of almost 30 community members from Nagaland, an Indian state bordering Myanmar, traveled to Britain for the handover.

The Pitt Rivers claims to hold the largest collection of Naga artifacts in the world, most of which were acquired by John Henry Hutton, an anthropologist and colonial administrator in the Indian Civil Service, and a second administrator/anthropologist, James Philip Mills, between 1910 and 1940. Within that collection, 219 items are listed as Naga human remains, including 39 ancestral human remains and 178 objects that contain, or may contain, human hair. (The current return concerns only the 39 ancestors.) Related Articles Sweden Returns Benin Bronze Trove to Nigeria, Indigenous Remains to Bolivia Museum Wiesbaden Will Be Germany's First Institution to Return Colonial-Era Human Remains to Indonesia

On its website, the museum notes that colonial “collecting” was “often bound up with the structures of colonial violence; remains and objects were frequently confiscated during punitive military expeditions, rather than freely given.” Mills, who wrote three monographs on Naga culture and practice, refers to at least one armed expedition to demand “headhunting trophies” from Pangsha village, remarking that the Pitt Rivers would thus “get some fine specimens.”

While the Naga people practiced headhunting at the time, taking the severed heads of enemies as trophies, they also kept and cared for the heads of their own chiefs. According to anthropologist Dolly Kikon (a member of Nagaland’s Lotha-Naga community, now at the University of California, Santa Cruz) one of the remains being returned is a chief’s skull.

The Pitt Rivers removed all human remains, including those of Naga people, from display in 2020. Sensing an opening, Kikon contacted the museum’s director, Laura Van Broekhoven and offered to connect her with the Naga community. The return is the culmination of the museum’s work with Naga partners, including the Forum for Naga Reconciliation (FNR) together with tribal Hohos, elders, churches and communities.

Naga tribal leaders plan to bury the remains together in a joint memorial park. “They’ve been here in a foreign land for more than a century,” said Dr Vihuto Asumi, President of the Sumî Hoho Naga tribe. “We believe that their spirits are still not at rest, so by doing this, we believe it will bring spiritual healing, not only to the ancestral remains here but to everyone who died during the headhunting days.”

Cucurella defends Vinicius Jr over Diomande comparison as Real Madrid pause eight-game winning run

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

By Alexis Pereira | 10 Nov 2026 20:52

Marc Cucurella has defended Vinicius Junior amid the Brazilian’s difficult start to the season, insisting that team-mate Yan Diomande does not help him more defensively than the Real Madrid forward does. Real Madrid have paused an eight-game winning run for the international break, during the best spell under boss Jose Mourinho, and are hoping Vinicius rediscovers his form with Brazil under Carlo Ancelotti. Cucurella shares that wish.

Speaking on the Cope radio programme El Partidazo, the Spanish left-back was asked about the poor start to the season of Vinicius, who has just one goal and three assists in 12 matches. The decline in his form, together with a suspension that kept him out of the last four La Liga games, has led to the meteoric rise of Diomande, who has made a difference on the left side.

Beyond four goals and four assists in 16 matches, the Ivory Coast forward has been praised for his tactical commitment, helping Cucurella with defensive recovery. But the Real Madrid number 17 rejects the idea that Diomande helps him more with marking than the Brazilian does.

‘I do not think he helps me more, I think he is more visible,’ Cucurella said. ‘Diomande runs back and is everywhere. Vinicius goes back three times and then, the fourth time he does not run, the ball is played in. If Vini scored three goals per game, nobody would say anything to him.’

Days after his signing from Chelsea was announced in late June, the defender told Radio Marca that he ‘did not mind’ if Vinicius ‘does not track back or help in defence’, because he wanted the number seven energised to have an impact in the final third with goals. Cucurella also told Vinicius that he would do all the ‘dirty work’ for him.

Despite that consent, the forward has helped with the compactness without the ball, following the Portuguese boss’s guidance. The main problem is that, in possession, the 26-year-old has not shown the same efficiency as in recent years in dribbles in one-on-one situations and in decision-making.

Like other figures at Real Madrid, the Spaniard came out in defence of Vinicius, considering a dip in form natural after consecutive seasons among the best players in the world. The left-back also insisted that Vinicius has been working behind the scenes to react and regain his prominence, which he considers fundamental to Mourinho’s success in 2026-27.

‘Vinicius is a player who has been performing at a spectacular level for five years, so it is understandable that he is going through a difficult phase now,’ Cucurella continued. ‘I see that he is motivated and works hard in training, and that is what matters.’

‘The most important thing for us is that he returns to his best level, because if he is at his peak, we have a great team,’ he concluded.

OpenAI, Anthropic face new oversight as New York’s AI safety law takes effect

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

New York is gearing up to enforce one of the country’s most significant AI safety laws. The state opened a registration portal Tuesday, with which large frontier AI developers like Anthropic and OpenAI will be required to register by January 1, 2027.

The RAISE Act, New York’s safety and transparency bill, kicks in at the start of the year. It mandates that large frontier developers — companies with more than $500 million in annual revenue — have to publish safety frameworks on their websites, report critical safety incidents within 72 hours, and issue public model transparency reports.

The newly formed Office of Digital Innovation, Governance, Integrity, and Trust (DIGIT) will implement the RAISE Act and oversee large frontier AI labs. Penalties can be as high as $1 million for one violation and $3 million for subsequent violations.

The state also can bring enforcement actions against companies that fail to register with the new portal, though a spokesperson declined to provide specifics.

“Tech moguls tell us that their vision for AI is inevitable,” Governor Kathy Hochul said Tuesday during a press briefing. “They tell us get on board or get left behind… That any collateral damage is worth it as long as we win the race to the AI cliff.”

On Tuesday, Hochul also launched the New York Center for AI Responsibility and Research based at State University of New York’s Binghamton University. The center will independently evaluate the safety, security, and operation of advanced AI systems and work with DIGIT to review companies’ safety frameworks. Hochul first announced the center in January with $55 million in donations and capital.

“We’re building a national model for smart, sensible AI regulation, a model that puts communities above corporations and people above powerful technology,” Hochul said.

The RAISE Act isn’t the only safety law New York has passed. Last year, the state began enforcing a law that requires AI companion operators to regularly let users know they’re talking to a bot, and to make reasonable efforts to detect suicidal ideation and self-harm.

In October, Hochul signed a separate chatbot safety law for minors, restricting behaviors like deceptive human-like interactions, emotionally manipulative or sycophantic responses, and encouragement of behaviors like suicide or disordered eating. That law takes effect June 1, 2027.

Other states have also passed similar AI safety and transparency bills to the RAISE Act. California’s SB 53, which took effect in January, mandates that frontier AI labs have in place and publish safety frameworks, enact whistleblower protections, and report critical safety incidents within 15 days. Illinois’ SB 315, which will go into effect at the start of 2027, has similar requirements, plus includes independent safety compliance audits.

Niclas Füllkrug credits Werder Bremen medical staff for helping him rediscover form

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Germany international Niclas Füllkrug has reflected on his return to Werder Bremen and explained why his familiar surroundings have helped him rediscover his best form following two challenging years abroad. The 33-year-old striker has enjoyed an outstanding start to the 2026/27 Bundesliga campaign, scoring nine league goals since returning to Werder Bremen from West Ham United during the summer transfer window.

Füllkrug’s performances have also earned him a recall to the German national team under Jürgen Klopp, bringing an end to an absence of more than a year. Speaking at Tuesday’s DFB press conference in Herzogenaurach, Füllkrug was asked to reflect on his experiences in England and Italy, where injuries and inconsistent playing time prevented him from establishing himself at West Ham and AC Milan.

The veteran centre-forward nevertheless rejected the suggestion that his time abroad had been entirely unsuccessful, emphasising the importance of his family and the positive experiences he enjoyed away from football. The particular adjective Füllkrug took issue with was “terrible”.

“Let me first emphasize that there was never a time in my life during which things were going ‘terrible’,” Füllkrug said in response to a question about his disappointing spell in England. “There are many components to life. Football is important, but my daughter and my family are central. I was able to take a lot from those years and build some fond memories with my family.”

“I simply couldn’t keep fit,” Füllkrug continued. “That’s all. I didn’t forget anything during these past two years, and I believe I’ve proven that now. I think the key was to be healthy and be on a team that passes to you in the crucial moments. Full praise to my teammates at Werder. I benefit enormously from the way the lads are playing the ball to me.”

Füllkrug’s return to Bremen has already proven to be one of the Bundesliga’s most successful summer moves. The striker quickly reestablished himself as a central figure in Werder’s attack, combining his goalscoring instincts with strong hold-up play, aerial ability, and an increasingly important role in the team’s attacking combinations.

His contributions have been particularly significant for Daniel Thioune’s side, with Füllkrug emerging as one of the Bundesliga’s leading goalscorers during the opening months of the campaign. Beyond the tactical and sporting considerations, however, Füllkrug believes that his longstanding personal connection to Werder has played a decisive role in helping him settle back into Bundesliga football.

“Werder is simply my home,” Füllkrug said when pressed further about how his performances had improved in a Werder shirt. “It’s my footballing home. As you all know, one sleeps the best in one’s own bed. I immediately felt comfortable there. I joined the academy at the age of 14 and, in my estimation, I believe about 70-90 percent of the same people are on staff there.”

“I’m healthy and there’s a medical department there that takes good care of me,” Füllkrug continued. “They’ve managed to get me back on track and ensure that I haven’t missed any training sessions. And we have a great team from which I benefit. The transfers that we brought in this summer have all done great. I just happen to be one of them.”

Remember PS5-exclusive shooter Fairgame$? It’s reportedly canceled now, to no one’s surprise

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Well, it seems like this one was only a matter of time.

Sony has reportedly canceled the long-missing online shooter Fairgame$ after it went missing in the public eye for years, according to Bloomberg’s Jason Schreier, adding to a long list of canceled PlayStation projects this generation.

Schreier said that “a recent test of the game with thousands of players did not deliver on the results PlayStation expected,” according to an internal email sent to employees today.

First revealed at PlayStation Showcase way back in May 2023, the game seemed to be themed around online heist PvP gameplay. But it hasn’t been seen since, and this cancellation comes as little surprise, as rumors about turmoil in development have been swirling for some time.

“While the test demonstrated strong operational excellence, live ops readiness and community engagement, player feedback and retention results were mixed and fell short of the goals we had set,” executives Sean Decker and John Doyle said in the memo. “Combined with challenging market conditions in the PVP shooter genre, we have made the difficult decision to cease development on the game.”

Earlier this year, it was reported that Fairgame$ was pivoting away from the “heist” mechanic and instead focusing on the extraction game type, similar to Bungie’s Marathon and Call of Duty’s DMZ. It apparently did not work out.

Fairgame$, developed by Haven Studios, joins a long list of PS5 projects that did not make the cut this generation as Sony incessantly chased live-service success. Concord launched and was quickly shuttered, while The Last of Us Factions, a God of War game, a Spider-Man game, Twisted Metal, and more never saw the light of day from PS5’s games-as-a-service initiative.

According to Schreier, “there will be no job cuts as a result of the cancellation,” and “Haven will instead move to support other studios within the organization.”

Trump Media posts $410M profit, but crypto hides an operating loss

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Trump Media reported $409.7 million in Q3 net income, driven largely by non-cash items, alongside $496.1 million in digital asset and equity gains.

Trump Media & Technology Group reported $409.7 million in net income for the third quarter of 2026, saying most of it reflected non-cash items, as gains on digital assets and equity securities drove the result.

The company announced the results for the quarter ended Sept. 30 in its Nov. 9 earnings release, furnished with a Form 8-K. It reported combined net realized and unrealized gains on digital assets and pledged digital assets and equity securities of $496.1 million.

Trump Media reported $3 million in quarterly revenue and a $12.3 million loss in adjusted earnings before interest, taxes, depreciation, and amortization (EBITDA).

The release's reconciliation and accompanying quarterly filing separately identify $378.1 million in realized and unrealized gains on digital assets and digital assets pledged, namely Bitcoin and Cronos.

Digital assets and pledged digital assets supplied most of the combined gain pool. Equity-security gains made up the rest; reported net income was $409.7 million.

The $378.1M digital-asset line includes pledged-asset revaluation, other digital-asset revaluation and derecognition gains.

Published unrealized and realized equity gains sum to $118.0M, rounded. The company says gains were driven primarily by bitcoin-related securities.

The $496.1M gain pool combines digital assets and securities. It is distinct from $409.7M in net income.

Realized, unrealized and derecognition gains are accounting categories, not equivalent cash proceeds. The gain pool is not a complete net-income bridge; other income and expenses also enter earnings.

Q3 2026 USD millions. Equity = 98.2999 + 19.6775 = 117.9774 (display 118.0). Total components = 496.1117 (release rounds 496.1). Main shares = 378.1343 / 496.1117 and 117.9774 / 496.1117, rounded to 76% / 24%. All component tracks use the same 496.1117 denominator; amount labels round to one decimal.

Realized, unrealized and derecognition gains are accounting categories, not equivalent cash proceeds. The gain pool is not a complete net-income bridge; other income and expenses also enter earnings.

The 10-Q breaks the digital asset line into about $200.8 million in unrealized gains on pledged digital assets, $107.5 million in unrealized gains on digital assets, and $69.9 million from derecognition of digital assets.

Thanks for signing up to The Catalyst. Look out for your first daily email. If you already receive it, you’re all set.

Derecognition means removing assets from the balance sheet, so the reported gains should not be read as cash generated during the quarter.

The 10-Q identifies about $98.3 million of unrealized equity gains and $19.7 million of realized equity gains, driven primarily by higher prices of bitcoin-related securities. Some of the investment gains outside the digital asset line also came from Bitcoin-linked exposure.

The release said gains were partially offset by $54 million in realized and unrealized derivative losses. Other highlighted offsets included $11.9 million in accreted interest expense, $9.4 million in stock-based compensation, and $4 million in investment expenses.

Trump Media said revenue rose 205% from a year earlier, reporting rounded figures of $3 million versus $1 million, and the 10-Q provides underlying amounts of approximately $3 million versus $972,900.

The filing attributes the increase to $1.3 million in subscription growth driven by Truth API and Truth+, about $523,300 in additional Truth Social advertising revenue, and $164,000 in Truth.Fi ETF management fees.

Those revenue gains sit alongside an adjusted EBITDA of a $12.3 million loss after adjustments for digital asset gains and losses, investment income and losses, net interest, taxes, depreciation and amortization, and stock-based compensation.

At Sept. 30, the company reported $2.5 billion in total assets and approximately $2.3 billion in what it calls financial assets. The latter is a non-GAAP basket spanning cash, restricted cash, short-term investments, equity securities, notes receivable and accrued interest, digital assets and pledged digital assets.

The company says financial assets should not be treated as a liquidity measure, and the basket is broader than a Bitcoin treasury total.

For the first nine months of 2026, the profitable third quarter still left a consolidated net loss of approximately $234.2 million. Trump Media also said it used $28.4 million in operating activities over the nine months ended Sept. 30, primarily because of expenses tied to recently concluded legacy legal matters.

PlayStation Cancels Fairgames After Years Of Silence

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Another one of PlayStation’s live-service games has crashed and burned, asÂ Fairgames, the company’s online shooter that was being developed by first-party PlayStation developer Haven Studios, has been canceled.

As reported by Bloomberg, Haven Studios isn’t undergoing any layoffs as a result of the cancelation, as “the studio will shift its focus to collaborating with other development teams across PlayStation Studios, while also exploring new creative opportunities,” according to a statement provided to the publication by PlayStation. The decision comes after Fairgames‘ recent playtest didn’t live up to Sony’s internal expectations.

âWhile the test demonstrated strong operational excellence, live ops readiness and community engagement, player feedback and retention results were mixed and fell short of the goals we had set,â PlayStation executives Sean Decker and John Doyle wrote in an email to employees and verified by Bloomberg. âCombined with challenging market conditions in the PVP shooter genre, we have made the difficult decision to cease development on the game.â

FairgamesÂ was initially announced back in 2023, and was originally being led by Jade Raymond, known as a producer of the earlyÂ Assassin’s Creed games. Raymond left Haven Studios in 2025, though the project quietly continued development. We really haven’t heard much about the game since it was initially unveiled.

Haven Studios was initially founded in 2021, and Sony acquired the company in 2022 before it had even put out a game. Now, it sounds like those who had been working onÂ FairgamesÂ will support the development of other games by PlayStation studios.

Fairgames is the latest in a long line of live-service disasters for Sony as the company continues to greenlight and then cancel its various attempts at forever games. Perhaps most famously, the company removed multiplayer shooter Concord from storefronts and then subsequently shut down its developer, Firewalk Studios.

Despite all this, Sony is still adamant that live-service games are an important part of PlayStation’s future.
